Now let me think about the plan for the demo second run. Actually, let me reconsider scope: the "改完再跑一遍" is a UI feature. For the offline demo to show the comparison, the demo seed would need a second session. But is that necessary? The e2e test can drive the rerun with fixtures — but the fixtures are recorded by scripted demo, and a second session with the revised resume text will produce different prompts → different digests → cassette missing in replay mode. So the e2e (which runs the server with fixtures dir + replay mode) would fail at the rerun step unless cassettes for the second session exist.

So there are two options:
1. Make e2e run the rerun in record mode with the scripted client — not possible over HTTP easily (mode live would need API key).
2. Add the second session to demo-seed so cassettes exist, AND make e2e drive the rerun with exactly the same revised resume text as seeded (deterministic). That's feasible: e2e clicks "全部采纳" then "改完再跑一遍" — the revised text comes from deterministic local computation from cassettes. So the prompt fingerprints match if the seed's second run used the same resume text and same panel/difficulty/style.

Hmm, but the seed would need to replicate the same "accept all" revision set. In demo-seed we can call acceptRevisions(db, firstId, allClaimIds) — same as UI's "全部采纳" then "确认采纳". The UI's accept-all chooses `candidates` = choosableClaimIds — need to check what that is vs acceptRevisions with all claim ids. Actually acceptRevisions(db, id, claimIds) where claimIds = all claims that have suggestions or supplements. The UI's "全部采纳" then "确认采纳" sends chosen ids. Let me check RevisionBar's onAcceptAll and App's chosen logic... `acceptAll(revised)` returns choosableClaimIds. That should equal all claims with suggestions.

Wait — but do the two paths produce the same `accepted`? acceptRevisions recomputes from session.resumeText with candidates from claim.suggestion + supplement. In seed we'd pass all claim ids (C1..C5). C1 has no suggestion → early skip "这条主张既没有改写建议，也没有补充说明" → fine, results in same applied set. Good.

Simpler: in seed, call acceptRevisions with the list of claim ids that have suggestions.

Then second session resume text = revised text. Second run must be recorded with scripted client, and replay must match.

But that doubles the cassette count for the 9 difficulty × style combos... Actually second run adds new prompts (different resume text → different extract prompt, etc.). For 9 combos × maybe ~10-15 cassettes = ~100+ new cassettes. Hmm, that's heavy but the earlier note said "C1 会让卡带涨到约 100 盘".

Alternatively: only seed the second run for the DEFAULT combination (常规档 × 常规风格). Then e2e (which uses default difficulty/style) can drive the rerun; other combos would lack cassettes for rerun → but users only hit that in live mode or... no, offline demo users can switch difficulty/style and then rerun → cassette missing error. But the demo error handling shows "找不到记录" message. Hmm, that's a bad experience for a judge.

Hmm. Wait. Let me think about how much the second-run prompts differ across difficulty/style:

- style changes suggest system → suggest cassettes differ.
So for all 9 combos, second run cassettes differ from first run. That's a lot.

Let me reconsider: maybe the scripted demo should produce, for the second run, an *identical* set of claims (same quotes) — no. The resume text changed so extract prompt changes → new cassette. Yes.

Cost estimate: current 56 cassettes. Second run per combo: extract 1, jd-extract 1, jd-merge 1, jd-match 1, probe ~3 rounds (but probe cassettes dedupe by digest; each round differs), answer ~10, verdict 5, suggest ~4, counter 1. But many are deduped across combos. Currently 56 across 9 combos because most cassettes are shared (difficulty affects probe/answer/verdict? style affects suggest only). Let's estimate second run adds ~30-40 unique cassettes → total ~90-100. That matches the earlier estimate.

But there's a big risk: the scripted demo's second run would produce claims from DEMO_PLANS but the resume text is the revised text; DEMO_PLANS quotes were the original sentences. Some quotes got rewritten by the suggestion `after` text. So `extractText` returns DEMO_PLANS quotes → resolveAnchor against the revised resume: C2's quote '主导引入 Redis 缓存，QPS 从 800 提升到 5000。' no longer exists (rewritten to '引入 Redis 缓存后，同一接口在预发环境单机压测下 QPS 由 800 提升到 5000（压测工具与口径见附录）'). So claims would be dropped → the second run would show most claims rejected. That would look broken.

So the scripted demo needs to be resume-aware: in the second run, produce claims with the *revised* quotes. That's what the earlier note concluded: make the script know about the rewritten text.

This is getting substantial. Let me think about a cheaper, more honest design:

**Option A (scripted demo gets a "second act"):** scripted-demo detects that the resume contains the rewritten sentences (e.g. substitute quotes: define `DEMO_PLANS` with `quoteAfter` — the quote as it appears after adopting the rewrite) and produces different verdicts (C2 → solid). <details><summary>🧠 思考过程</summary>

Now I have the full demo-seed. The task: build the second run in the offline demo so the compare feature is demonstrable offline.

Design: In `runOne`, after the record run, do a second run:
1. Accept revisions on the record DB (need `acceptRevisions` from revisions.ts) for claim ids that have suggestions (e.g. C2..C5). Actually more precisely: accept all suggestions.
2. Then create a second session with parentSessionId = first id, resumeText = revised text, and run again with the same scripted client... but wait: the scripted client has ScriptState with `asked` set — reuse means no probes in second run (documented bug). So the second run needs a **fresh** scripted client (fresh ScriptState) but same cassette dir. That's fine — entries dedupe by digest.

But careful: the second run's prompts differ (resume text changed), so digests differ → more cassettes. That's expected; cassette count will grow.

Now the scripted demo must produce different verdicts in second run. The scripted planner keys on claimId ('C'+n index into DEMO_PLANS). In the second run, extraction will run on the revised resume text. The extractText function returns DEMO_PLANS five + unanchored draft regardless of resume. But the anchoring gate will drop claims whose quote isn't in the revised resume text. If C2's quote was rewritten (after text), the C2 quote no longer exists → C2 draft gets rejected as unanchored. Hmm. That's a problem: after accepting revisions, quotes C2..C5 change, so extract claims quoting old text get dropped → second run only has C1 + maybe others unchanged.

Wait: extractText returns quote = plan.quote (original). After rewrite, C2 quote changed to the conservative after text. So in run 2, C2's claim would be rejected as unanchored. Only C1 remains anchored (no suggestion). So second run has 1 claim (C1) — poor demo.

Better design for the demo: make `extractText` aware of the resume text in the prompt — i.e., read which quotes are present in the prompt's resume section and emit the *rewritten* quote for claims whose quote is absent but whose rewritten text is present. That's a bigger change to scripted-demo.

Alternative simpler design: second run resumes from the **revised** text, and extract returns plans whose quote is the revised text when present. Let me think about how to implement deterministically and honestly.

Approach: add to scripted-demo a helper that, given the extract prompt (which contains the resume text lines and the target JD), produces claims by matching each DEMO_PLAN:
- If plan.quote appears in prompt → use plan.quote (first run behavior).
- Else if an "after" variant (suggestion.after, or afterByStyle values) appears in prompt → use that as quote, and mark the claim as "already fixed" so that in probe/verdict stages it produces a solid verdict.

But `planForClaimId` maps by claim id and order. In run 2, claims order changes. We need per-request context: the verdict prompt contains `原文引文：<quote>` — so `verdictText` can look up the plan by quote rather than by claim id! Actually `claimIdForQuote` exists for counters. There's a pattern: extract quote from prompt then find which plan matches.

Simplest robust approach: make lookups quote-based with a fallback to claim-id-based. Define `planForQuote(quote)` that matches plan.quote, or any afterByStyle/after text; returns {plan, fixed: boolean}. Then:
- `answerText`: currently `lineAfter('## 面试官的问题')` then planForQuestion by question text; question text stays the same in run 2 (probeText uses plan.questions). But if the claim is "fixed", the demo should answer the question fully (answered true). Hmm—for C2 in run 2, the question was "QPS 从 800 到 5000 的口径是什么" and after rewriting the resume now says "（前后口径不同，不作直接对比）" — the candidate can now answer by citing it. That's a nice demo: answer cites the new quote.
- `verdictText`: for fixed claim → solid with evidenceQuotes = [new quote]; else plan.verdict.

But how does answer/verdict know which claim/quote is in play? The answer prompt has '## 针对的主张' with renderClaim including quote. Verdict prompt has '## 待判定主张' + renderClaim. So quote extraction from prompt is feasible: find lines '原文引文：<...>'.

Let me look at the answer/verdict prompt structure and the existing extract functions to write this precisely. Let me read scripted-demo.ts sections: 346-560 and 748-826. Also demo-content resume text to know exact strings.

Actually — hold on. Let me reconsider scope. This is a big change. Is offline demo second-run essential? Consider: the offline demo is what judges see without API keys. C1's value ("改完再跑一遍，判定怎么变") would be invisible offline. The user's requirement #8 and C1 are core. I think yes, it's worth doing — it also produces the e2e assertion evidence offline.

Alternative cheaper approach for the demo: **seed the second session as a separate top-level demo run** rather than inside the 9 combos. I.e., in `seedDemoCassettes`, after the nine combos, perform one extra "版本对比" demo using the default difficulty/style: run first session (already have it? no, runOne closes dbs). Now I have what I need. Let me plan the demo second-run implementation.

Goal: offline demo (seed) should produce a second session that demonstrates "改完再跑一遍". The seed currently runs one session per (difficulty × style) combination. Adding a second session per combination doubles the cassette count potentially — but cassettes are addressed by prompt digest. The second session differs in: resume text (revised), parentSessionId, and claims extracted from the revised resume.

Important: extract stage prompt contains resume text → digest differs → new cassette needed. All downstream stages (probe/answer/verdict/suggest/jd-match...) also contain resume text → new digests. The demo seed only pre-records for the built-in demo. In replay mode, a user clicking "改完再跑一遍" on the demo session would need cassettes for the second run with the same difficulty/style they chose. If they chose a non-default combo, cassettes would be missing → error.

Hmm. So to be consistent with the existing design principle ("用户在界面上切一下档位，点开跑就报找不到记录"), we should record the second run for all 9 combos. That's the principled choice.

Let me check: buildJdExtractPrompt — contains JD text only, so identical across runs → same digest, no new cassette!). Similarly jd-merge (contains extracted requirements from jd-extract, which are the same) → same digest. jd-match contains claims → differs. extract contains resume → differs. probe contains claims + resume → differs. answer contains resume + question → differs. verdict → differs. suggest → differs. counter → differs (resume + claims).

So the second run adds roughly: extract 1 + jd-match 1 + probe N + answer M + verdict K + suggest J + counter 1. In the first run 56 = extract1 + jd-extract1 + jd-merge1 + jd-match1 + probe24 + answer10 + verdict5 + suggest12 + counter1. That's the crux: the scripted extractor is static. For the second run, the scripted demo must return claims whose quotes exist in the (revised) resume text. So the script needs to detect that the resume contains the revised C2 sentence and emit the revised quote for C2.

Simplest robust approach: make `extractText(prompt)` inspect the resume section of the prompt. For each plan, choose quote: if plan.quote appears in prompt → use it; else if a rewritten variant (from suggestion.afterByStyle values ∪ after) appears in prompt → use that variant as quote, and mark state `revised=true` for that claim. Then verdict for that claim = solid with evidence.

Then downstream: probe stage uses claimBlocks(prompt) which reads the actual rendered claims from the prompt → claim quotes are the revised ones automatically. But careful: many moving parts; script state (asked set) is per client. In the seed, each combination creates a fresh client and runs ONE session. If we add a second session, we should create a second fresh client for the second session so its `asked` set is empty — otherwise no probes at all (the known bug).

Practical approach: derive the second-run plan set *from the revised resume text itself*. The script can read the resume section from the extract prompt and find which of the known "after" sentences are present. Then produce claims accordingly:

So the script needs: given a prompt (which contains the resume and the claim quote), determine for a plan whether we're in "revised" mode. Concretely:

```ts
function revisedAfter(plan: DemoClaimPlan, prompt: string): string | null {
  const s = plan.suggestion;
  if (s === null) return null;
  // 简历里的原句已经变成了哪一档的改写版？
  const candidates = [s.after, ...Object.values(s.afterByStyle)];
  for (const text of candidates) if (prompt.indexOf(text) >= 0) return text;
  return null;
}
```

<details><summary>🧠 思考过程</summary>

Now, let me think about the design for the demo's second run.

Goal: offline demo (replay mode, no API key) should also be able to demonstrate "改完再跑一遍" comparison. But wait — how does the demo work in the UI? User clicks "示例简历" → demo loaded → runs a session. Then user can accept revisions → click "改完再跑一遍" → this calls startRun with revised.text and parentSessionId.

In replay mode, the cassette lookup is keyed on prompt fingerprint. The second run's resume text is different (revised), so extract prompt differs → new cassette digests. That would require recording cassettes for the second run too.

Hmm, this is heavy. But wait — is it needed? The e2e uses replay mode with `CROSSEXAM_FIXTURES=D:/CZB/fixtures/llm`. If e2e's C1 assertion is just "第一版" + button presence, we don't need a working second run in demo. But the user wants a real working feature; for the competition demo video, they'd likely use live mode.

But it's better if the offline demo supports it, since评委 will try it offline. If judge clicks "改完再跑一遍" in replay mode and gets "cassette-missing" error, that's bad.

Let me think about the minimal viable approach: The second run's prompts differ from first run's prompts because the resume text changed. All stages depend on resume text: extract, probe (resume text + claims), answer, verdict, suggest, counter, and jd-match? JD stages depend on JD text only... jd-extract depends on JD only — same fingerprint → reused. jd-merge depends on extracted requirements — same. jd-match depends on requirements + claims — claims may differ if extract produced different claims.

So the second run needs its own set of cassettes for extract/probe/answer/verdict/suggest/counter/jd-match. That's potentially ~29 more cassettes. Recording them requires the scripted demo client to handle the second-run prompts.

Complexity: the scripted client's probe/answer/verdict logic keys off claim quotes. In the second run, the revised resume contains the rewritten sentences. Would extract still produce the same 5 claims? The scripted extractText always returns DEMO_PLANS 5 claims + unanchored draft, regardless of prompt. But the anchoring gate requires quote to exist in the resume text. If C2's quote `主导引入 Redis 缓存，QPS 从 800 提升到 5000。` no longer exists in the revised resume (it was replaced by the after text), then that claim gets rejected — which would kill the demo.

So we need scripted extractText to be aware of the resume text in the prompt and emit quotes that actually exist. And verdicts to reflect the improvement.

This is a substantial piece of work. But it's the core "wow" of C1: 改完再跑一遍，判定从「表述过度」变成「站得住」.

Hmm. Let me reconsider scope. Is there a cheaper design that's still honest?
